     9	// RED — Layer-1 supervised-child no-op guard (subtask l1-supervised-guard).
    10	//
    11	// Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
    12	//   §"Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
    13	//   finding 1)" and §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 1.
    14	//
    15	// Gate-1 findings this traces to:
    16	//   - iteration-2 finding 1: Layer-2 main-root children collide with the
    17	//     supervisor-owned bundle; the fix is a BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD no-op guard
    18	//     on the hook entrypoints AND `baton checkpoint` so children never touch
    19	//     any bundle (no write, no foreign-session rejection).
    20	//   - iteration-3 finding 1: the guard originally MISSED `session-start`, which
    21	//     would still read the supervisor-owned bundle and inject pending-handoff
    22	//     resume context into the child. The fix extends the guard to EVERY
    23	//     hook-invoked command — `session-start` included — asserting zero writes
    24	//     AND zero resume context.
    25	//
    26	// CONTRACT (pinned here):
    27	//   G1. ACTIVATION. `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` set to ANY non-empty string
    28	//       activates the guard. Unset OR empty string ('') is NORMAL behavior.
    29	//   G2. SILENCE. Under the guard, every hook-invoked command exits 0 with
    30	//       stdout === '' and stderr === '' — even with --json (no envelope).
    31	//   G3. NO .handoff/ TOUCH. Under the guard, the command reads NOTHING under
    32	//       `<root>/.handoff/` (bundle, journal, lock, log) and writes NOTHING
    33	//       anywhere. The no-read property is the security contract (mirrors the
    34	//       symlink jail test, tests/commands/gate2-iter3-group6.test.mjs F12):
    35	//       an implementation that reads the bundle then suppresses output still
    36	//       leaks the supervisor bundle to the child and fails here.
    37	//   G4. GUARD-BEFORE-PARSING (ambiguity resolved — see note below). The guard
    38	//       check runs FIRST, before flag parsing. So a garbled invocation (a
    39	//       stray positional) or a missing required flag STILL produces the silent
    40	//       exit-0 no-op instead of a usage error. Pinned per command.
    41	//
    42	// CONTRACT AMBIGUITY RESOLVED: the plan says the guard "quietly no-ops (exit 0,
    43	// empty output)" but does not state whether it precedes flag parsing. Two
    44	// readings were possible: (a) parse first, then guard (a bad invocation exits 2
    45	// even for a supervised child); (b) guard first (a supervised child is ALWAYS
    46	// silent). We pinned (b) — the simpler, safer contract: a supervised child must
    47	// NEVER emit anything on stdout/stderr or set a nonzero exit, because the
    48	// supervisor spawns these hooks and any output/exit is noise it must not have
    49	// to filter. The guard is the very first statement of each command.
    50	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    51	
    52	const T0 = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
    53	const paths = bundlePaths('/repo');
    54	const HANDOFF_PREFIX = '/repo/.handoff';
    55	
    56	const snapText = (/** @type {any} */ b) => JSON.stringify(b, null, 2) + '\n';
    57	const event = (/** @type {any} */ obj) => JSON.stringify({ schema: 'baton/event@1', ...obj });
    58	
    59	function checkpointBundle(overrides = {}) {
    60	  return {

    81	// A foreign, sealed bundle: the state that NORMALLY makes session-start emit a
    82	// pending-handoff resume notice on stdout (proven at its home in
    83	// tests/commands/session-start.test.mjs). Under the guard it must emit nothing.
    84	function foreignSealedBundle() {
    85	  return checkpointBundle({
    86	    bundleId: 'b_guardss00000000',
    87	    origin: { platform: 'codex', model: 'm', sessionHint: 's', unstable: false },
    88	    handoff: { status: 'sealed', reason: 'r', reasonClass: 'usage-limit', toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: T0, receive_log: [] },
    89	  });
    90	}

    92	/**
    93	 * Instrument io.fs: record the first read of any path under `<root>/.handoff/`
    94	 * and every mutating call. Mirrors the readFileSync spy idiom in
    95	 * tests/commands/gate2-iter3-group6.test.mjs (F12) and uses the write methods
    96	 * the memfs fake exposes (writeFileSync / appendFileSync / mkdirSync /
    97	 * renameSync). __history is a defensive backstop over the same mutations.
    98	 * @param {any} io
    99	 */
   100	function spyFs(io) {
   101	  const state = { readUnderHandoff: /** @type {string | null} */ (null), writes: /** @type {string[]} */ ([]) };
   102	
   103	  const realRead = io.fs.readFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   104	  io.fs.readFileSync = (/** @type {string} */ p, /** @type {any} */ enc) => {
   105	    if (state.readUnderHandoff === null && String(p).startsWith(HANDOFF_PREFIX)) state.readUnderHandoff = String(p);
   106	    return realRead(p, enc);
   107	  };
   108	
   109	  const realWrite = io.fs.writeFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   110	  io.fs.writeFileSync = (/** @type {string} */ p, /** @type {any} */ data, /** @type {any} */ enc) => {
   111	    state.writes.push(String(p));
   112	    return realWrite(p, data, enc);
   113	  };
   114	  const realAppend = io.fs.appendFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   115	  io.fs.appendFileSync = (/** @type {string} */ p, /** @type {any} */ data) => {
   116	    state.writes.push(String(p));
   117	    return realAppend(p, data);
   118	  };
   119	  const realMkdir = io.fs.mkdirSync.bind(io.fs);
   120	  io.fs.mkdirSync = (/** @type {string} */ p, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
   121	    state.writes.push(String(p));
   122	    return realMkdir(p, opts);
   123	  };
   124	  const realRename = io.fs.renameSync.bind(io.fs);
   125	  io.fs.renameSync = (/** @type {string} */ from, /** @type {string} */ to) => {
   126	    state.writes.push(String(to));
   127	    return realRename(from, to);
   128	  };
   129	
   130	  return state;
   131	}
   132	
   133	/** The three silence assertions common to every guard-active case (G2). */
   134	function assertSilent(io, code, label) {
   135	  assert.equal(code, 0, `${label}: guard active exits 0`);
   136	  assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), '', `${label}: guard active writes nothing to stdout (not even a --json envelope)`);
   137	  assert.equal(io.stderrText(), '', `${label}: guard active writes nothing to stderr`);
   138	}

     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
     4	import { bundlePaths } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';
     5	// SOLE RED CAUSE for this file: the module below does not exist yet, so this
     6	// static import throws ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND at load and every test in the file
     7	// reds with that single, unambiguous cause. (core/* and helper imports above all
     8	// resolve — only hook.mjs is missing.)
     9	import { runHook } from '../../adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs';
    10	
    11	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12	// ADAPTERS wave — RED. Behavioural contract for the Claude Code single hook
    13	// entrypoint, exercised over injected io (mirrors the core command tests:
    14	// cmdX(args, io) -> exit code). The adapter CALLS the core CLI — these tests pin
    15	// the adapter SURFACE only (event→command mapping, the SessionStart context
    16	// shape, fail-open), never core checkpoint/detect logic.
    17	//
    18	// TARGET MODULE: adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs (sole target).
    19	//
    20	// Source of truth: plan §Claude Code adapter hook table + platform-notes
    21	// §Claude Code (SessionStart injects hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext).
    22	//
    23	// PINNED CONTRACT (open details resolved here):
    24	//   S0. SIGNATURE. `runHook(args, io) => Promise<number>`, where args[0] is the
    25	//       hook event name (passed by hooks.json) and io is the injected object
    26	//       {cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now, …}. The hook payload
    27	//       arrives as the STRING io.stdin (Claude writes hook JSON on stdin).
    28	//   S1. BATON REACH. To run a core command, hook.mjs invokes the core CLI as a
    29	//       child process via io.execFile: it resolves the baton bin as
    30	//       `${io.env.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs` and calls
    31	//       io.execFile(node, [batonBin, <subcmd>, …flags], {input}). Tests assert on
    32	//       the recorded invocation. (Verifier fold V1) EVERY core child call —
    33	//       checkpoint AND detect — must use that resolved bin path and must forward
    34	//       the raw hook payload as the child's stdin: opts.input === io.stdin. An
    35	//       adapter that bypasses the core CLI or drops the payload cannot pass.
    36	//   S2. MAPPING. Stop / PreCompact / SessionEnd -> exactly one `checkpoint
    37	//       --platform claude-code` invocation, identifying the source event.
    38	//       StopFailure -> BOTH a `detect --platform claude-code` invocation (the
    39	//       classify step) AND a `checkpoint --platform claude-code` invocation
    40	//       stamped with a stop-failure trigger; a rate_limit StopFailure carries the
    41	//       rate_limit signal through to the checkpoint (marks the bundle limit-hit).
    42	//   S3. SessionStart is NOT a checkpoint: it spawns NO checkpoint child. Instead
    43	//       it reads <cwd>/.handoff/bundle.json (read-only, via io.fs) and, when a
    44	//       PENDING (sealed OR usage-limit) bundle FROM ANOTHER PLATFORM exists, emits
    45	//       exactly the Claude context shape on stdout:
    46	//         {"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart",
    47	//                                  "additionalContext":"…suggest /baton:receive…"}}
    48	//       (Verifier fold V2) The full pending matrix is pinned:
    49	//         positive:  foreign SEALED bundle            -> context
    50	//         positive:  foreign OPEN + reasonClass usage-limit (limit death before
    51	//                    finalize — the primary failure case) -> context
    52	//         negative:  foreign OPEN non-limit bundle    -> no context
    53	//         negative:  own-platform open non-limit      -> no context
    54	//         negative:  no bundle at all                 -> no context
    55	//       proving BOTH disjuncts of "sealed OR limit-hit" AND "foreign only".
    56	//   S4. FAIL-OPEN. Every error path (unparseable stdin, a rejected core
    57	//       invocation, a missing/corrupt bundle) returns 0 and never throws — a hook
    58	//       must never break the host session. Claude ignores the exit code anyway.
    59	//
    60	// "limit-hit" (S2/S3): pinned to the documented handoff fields — a bundle whose
    61	// handoff.status==='sealed' OR handoff.reasonClass==='usage-limit' is "pending".
    62	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    63	
    64	const T0 = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
    65	const paths = bundlePaths('/repo');
    66	const PLUGIN_ROOT = '/repo';
    67	
    68	/**
    69	 * Build an io whose execFile RECORDS every invocation. `respond` lets a test
    70	 * control the child's result (resolve/reject); default resolves empty.
    71	 */
    72	function makeHookIo({ stdin = '', files = {}, respond } = {}) {
    73	  const io = makeIo({ env: { CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT: PLUGIN_ROOT }, stdin, files, now: T0 });
    74	  const calls = [];
    75	  io.execFile = (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => {
    76	    calls.push({ cmd, args: Array.isArray(args) ? args : [], opts: opts || {} });
    77	    if (respond) return respond({ cmd, args, opts });
    78	    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
    79	  };
    80	  return { io, calls };
    81	}
    82	
    83	const line = (c) => [c.cmd, ...c.args].join(' ');
    84	const inputOf = (c) => (typeof c.opts.input === 'string' ? c.opts.input : '');
    85	const serialize = (c) => `${line(c)} ${inputOf(c)}`;
    86	const findSub = (calls, sub) => calls.filter((c) => c.args.includes(sub) || new RegExp(`\\b${sub}\\b`).test(line(c)));
    87	
    88	const BATON_BIN = `${PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs`;
    89	/** (V1) Every core child call goes through the core CLI with the payload forwarded. */
    90	function assertBatonCall(c, io, label) {
    91	  assert.ok(line(c).includes(BATON_BIN), `${label}: the invocation uses the baton bin resolved under CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT (${BATON_BIN}); got: ${line(c)}`);
    92	  assert.equal(inputOf(c), io.stdin, `${label}: the raw hook payload is forwarded as the child's stdin (opts.input === io.stdin)`);
    93	}

   100	    createdAt: T0,
   101	    updatedAt: T0,
   102	    origin: { platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5', sessionHint: 'sess-cc', unstable: false },
   103	    task: { goal: 'g', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
   104	    plan: { steps: [] },
   105	    decisions: [],
   106	    files: { touched: [] },
   107	    roles: { assignments: {} },
   108	    git: null,
   109	    handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
   110	    journalSeq: 0,
   111	    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
   112	    dedupeRing: [],
   113	    ...overrides,
   114	  };

   296	  // Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
   297	  // §"Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3, finding 1)"
   298	  // + §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 1. When the supervisor sets
   299	  // BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD in a child's env, the hook entrypoint must fast-path
   300	  // no-op for EVERY event it handles WITHOUT spawning any baton child process:
   301	  // exit 0, empty stdout/stderr, and — critically — io.execFile is NEVER called
   302	  // (a spawned child would itself read/write the supervisor-owned bundle, which

   315	      assert.equal(io.stderrText(), '', `${event}: no stderr under the guard`);
   316	    }
   317	  });

     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
     6	//
     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.
    20	
    21	import { makeMemfs } from './memfs.mjs';
    22	
    23	export function makeIo({
    24	  files = {},
    25	  env = {},
    26	  stdin = '',
    27	  execResults = {},
    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
    29	  host = 'host-A',
    30	  pid = 4242,
    31	  startTime = 111000,
    32	  processAlive = null,
    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
    34	  execPath = process.execPath,
    35	} = {}) {
    36	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
    37	  const outChunks = [];
    38	  const errChunks = [];
    39	  let currentNow = now;
    40	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
    41	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
    42	
    43	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    44	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    45	
    46	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
    47	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
    48	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    49	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
    50	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
    51	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
    52	        return reject(e);
    53	      }
    54	      const r = execResults[key];
    55	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
    56	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
    57	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
    58	      if (code !== 0) {
    59	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
    60	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
    61	        e.code = code;
    62	        e.stdout = out;
    63	        e.stderr = err;
    64	        return reject(e);
    65	      }
    66	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
    67	    });
    68	  };
    69	
    70	  return {
    71	    cwd: '/repo',
    72	    env,
    73	    stdin,
    74	    stdout,
    75	    stderr,
    76	    fs,
    77	    execFile,
    78	    now: () => currentNow,
    79	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
    80	    execPath,
    81	    host,
    82	    pid,
    83	    startTime,
    84	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
    85	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
    86	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
    87	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
    88	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
    89	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
    90	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
    91	    files: filesSnapshot,
    92	  };
    93	}

    12	// Snapshot rewrite throttle: journal append ALWAYS; the snapshot (and
    13	// HANDOFF.md) re-render only on an important event type, >30 s since the last
    14	// snapshot, or >=10 accreted events (plan §Checkpoint engine).
    15	const ROUTINE_TYPES = new Set(['note', 'file.touch']);
    16	const THROTTLE_WINDOW_MS = 30_000;
    17	const THROTTLE_EVENT_COUNT = 10;

    19	// Transcript tail bounds (plan §Transcript policy): last 10 messages, 8 KB cap,
    20	// opt-in via baton.config.json capture.transcriptTail, PreCompact only.
    21	const TAIL_MESSAGES = 10;
    22	const TAIL_BYTES = 8192;
    23	
    24	/**
    25	 * Opt-in transcript-tail capture (gate-2 fix 10). Returns the bounded,
    26	 * redacted tail or null when the gate is closed: config off (the default),
    27	 * not a PreCompact payload, no usable transcript_path, or an unreadable /
    28	 * symlinked file. The path comes from an untrusted hook payload — it is read
    29	 * only under explicit opt-in, must lstat as a regular file, and its content
    30	 * passes the secret-redaction filter before storage.
    31	 * @param {string} root @param {any} raw parsed hook payload @param {any} io
    32	 * @returns {string | null}
    33	 */
    34	function captureTranscriptTail(root, raw, io) {
    35	  const cfg = safeReadJson(io.fs, `${root}/baton.config.json`);
    36	  if (!cfg.ok || cfg.value?.capture?.transcriptTail !== true) return null;
    37	
    38	  const eventName = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? (raw.hook_event_name ?? raw.event) : null;
    39	  if (typeof eventName !== 'string' || !/precompact/i.test(eventName)) return null;
    40	  const path = raw.transcript_path;
    41	  if (typeof path !== 'string' || path.length === 0) return null;
    42	
    43	  try {
    44	    if (!io.fs.lstatSync(path).isFile()) return null;
    45	    // Allowlist the transcript path (gate-2 iter-2 m1): the payload is
    46	    // untrusted, so its resolved realpath must sit under the repo root, the
    47	    // user's ~/.claude tree (where Claude Code actually writes transcripts),
    48	    // or a configured capture.transcriptDir — never an arbitrary readable file.
    49	    const fileReal = io.fs.realpathSync(path);
    50	    // On POSIX a backslash is a LEGAL filename character, not a separator, so
    51	    // normalizing '\\'→'/' in the containment check (isContained) could make an
    52	    // out-of-tree sibling like '/repo\\evil/f' appear contained (iter-4 I9).
    53	    // The transcript path is the one fully-untrusted path that reaches
    54	    // containment, so refuse a backslash-bearing realpath off Windows.
    55	    if (io.platform !== 'win32' && String(fileReal).includes('\\')) return null;
    56	    /** @type {string[]} */
    57	    const allowed = [root];
    58	    const home = io.env?.HOME;
    59	    if (typeof home === 'string' && home.length > 0) allowed.push(`${home}/.claude`);
    60	    const dir = cfg.value?.capture?.transcriptDir;
    61	    if (typeof dir === 'string' && dir.length > 0) allowed.push(dir);
    62	    const under = (/** @type {string} */ base) => {
    63	      let baseReal;
    64	      try {
    65	        baseReal = io.fs.realpathSync(base);
    66	      } catch {
    67	        baseReal = base;
    68	      }
    69	      // Compare on normalized forward-slash spellings (iter-3 F9): raw Windows
    70	      // backslash realpaths never satisfy a forward-slash containment, so
    71	      // opt-in capture would silently never fire.
    72	      return isContained(baseReal, fileReal);
    73	    };
    74	    if (!allowed.some(under)) return null;
    75	
    76	    const text = io.fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
    77	    const lines = text.split('\n').filter((/** @type {string} */ l) => l.trim() !== '');
    78	    let tail = lines.slice(-TAIL_MESSAGES).join('\n');
    79	    if (Buffer.byteLength(tail) > TAIL_BYTES) {
    80	      tail = Buffer.from(tail).subarray(-TAIL_BYTES).toString('utf8');
    81	    }
    82	    return redactSecrets(tail);
    83	  } catch {
    84	    return null; // unreadable transcript is never a checkpoint failure
    85	  }
    86	}
    87	
    88	/** Event-shaped: an object carrying a string `type`. */
    89	const isEventShaped = (/** @type {any} */ v) => v !== null && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v) && typeof v.type === 'string';
    90	
    91	/**
    92	 * Tolerant stdin parse (live /baton:handoff failure: a model sent one event
    93	 * per line and the whole narrative checkpoint silently failed). Accept the
    94	 * canonical single JSON value, a bare array of events, or NDJSON where every
    95	 * line is an event object or a {schema, events} wrapper. All-or-nothing: one
    96	 * non-event element fails the whole parse — never a partial apply.
    97	 * @param {string} text @returns {{ok: true, raw: any} | {ok: false, why?: string}}
    98	 */
    99	function parseStdin(text) {
   100	  let single;
   101	  let singleOk = false;
   102	  try {
   103	    single = JSON.parse(text);
   104	    singleOk = true;
   105	  } catch {
   106	    singleOk = false;
   107	  }
   108	  if (singleOk) {
   109	    if (Array.isArray(single)) {
   110	      return single.every(isEventShaped) ? { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events: single } } : { ok: false };
   111	    }
   112	    // A closest-miss of the documented wrapper — an events array whose schema
   113	    // key is missing or wrong — must ERROR, never silently degrade to a junk
   114	    // note with ok:true (the model cannot self-correct from a success
   115	    // envelope while its narrative was discarded).
   116	    if (single !== null && typeof single === 'object' && Array.isArray(single.events) && single.schema !== 'baton/event@1') {
   117	      return { ok: false, why: `stdin carries an events array but schema is ${single.schema === undefined ? 'missing' : JSON.stringify(single.schema)} — use {"schema":"baton/event@1","events":[…]}` };
   118	    }
   119	    // A single bare event object (string `type`, none of the raw-hook payload
   120	    // markers) is one of the two most likely readings of "pipe events on
   121	    // stdin" — accept it as that event instead of degrading it to a junk note.
   122	    if (
   123	      isEventShaped(single) &&
   124	      single.schema === undefined &&
   125	      !('hook_event_name' in single) &&
   126	      !('session_id' in single) &&
   127	      !('event' in single)
   128	    ) {
   129	      return { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events: [single] } };
   130	    }
   131	    return { ok: true, raw: single };
   132	  }
   133	  const lines = text.split('\n').map((l) => l.trim()).filter((l) => l !== '');
   134	  if (lines.length === 0) return { ok: false };
   135	  /** @type {any[]} */
   136	  const events = [];
   137	  for (const line of lines) {
   138	    let v;
   139	    try {
   140	      v = JSON.parse(line);
   141	    } catch {
   142	      return { ok: false };
   143	    }
   144	    if (v && typeof v === 'object' && v.schema === 'baton/event@1' && Array.isArray(v.events)) {
   145	      if (!v.events.every(isEventShaped)) return { ok: false };
   146	      events.push(...v.events);
   147	    } else if (isEventShaped(v)) {
   148	      events.push(v);
   149	    } else {
   150	      return { ok: false };
   151	    }
   152	  }
   153	  return { ok: true, raw: { schema: 'baton/event@1', events } };
   154	}
   155	
   156	/**
   157	 * `baton checkpoint` — mechanical checkpoint from a hook payload on stdin.
   158	 * Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on every soft failure so a checkpoint can never
   159	 * break the host harness; `--strict` opts into hard failures (CI).
   160	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
   161	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   162	 */
   163	export async function cmdCheckpoint(args, io) {
   164	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string', model: 'string', trigger: 'string', debounce: 'string', strict: 'boolean', 'take-over': 'boolean' });
   165	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'checkpoint', parsed.error);
   166	  const flags = parsed.flags;
   167	  const strict = flags.strict === true;
   168	  const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
   169	  if (!platform) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', '--platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required');
   170	  const pErr = platformError(platform);
   171	  if (pErr) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', pErr);
   172	
   173	  try {
   174	    return await run(flags, platform, io);
   175	  } catch (err) {
   176	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
   177	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'checkpoint-failed', msg } });
   178	    io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: ${msg}\n`);
   179	    return strict ? 1 : 0;
   180	  }
   181	}
   182	
   183	/**
   184	 * @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
   185	 * @param {string} platform
   186	 * @param {any} io
   187	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   188	 */
   189	async function run(flags, platform, io) {
   190	  const strict = flags.strict === true;
   191	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   192	  const paths = bundlePaths(root);
   193	  // The --json contract (gate-2 fix 11): exactly one envelope on stdout for
   194	  // every exit path — the exit code stays governed by hook-safety.
   195	  const finish = (/** @type {any} */ env, /** @type {number} */ code) => {
   196	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, env);
   197	    return code;
   198	  };
   199	
   200	  const parsed = parseStdin(typeof io.stdin === 'string' ? io.stdin : '');
   201	  if (!parsed.ok) {
   202	    const why = parsed.why ?? 'send one JSON value ({"schema":"baton/event@1","events":[…]}), a JSON array of events, or NDJSON event lines';
   203	    io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: stdin could not be parsed — ${why}\n`);
   204	    return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'bad-stdin', msg: why } }, strict ? 1 : 0);
   205	  }
   206	  const raw = parsed.raw;
   207	
   208	  const session = { host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime };
   209	  // The hook command declares which event it is (--trigger), so event identity
   210	  // never depends on parsing each harness's payload shape (Cursor's stop payload
   211	  // names the event nowhere normalize could find it).
   212	  const explicitEvent = typeof flags.trigger === 'string' ? flags.trigger : undefined;
   213	  const events = normalizeHookPayload(raw, platform, session, explicitEvent);
   214	  if (events.length === 0) return finish({ ok: true, data: { events: 0, rewritten: false } }, 0);
   215	
   216	  // Opt-in transcript tail (gate-2 fix 10): rides the journal as its own
   217	  // IMPORTANT event so the snapshot rewrite below persists it, and purge can
   218	  // strip it from every retained copy by its property name.
   219	  const tail = captureTranscriptTail(root, raw, io);
   220	  if (tail !== null) {
   221	    events.push({
   222	      type: 'transcript.set',
   223	      payload: { transcript: { capturedAt: io.now(), tail } },
   224	      source: platform,
   225	      sessionHint: events[0]?.sessionHint,
   226	      unstable: events[0]?.unstable ?? true,
   227	    });
   228	  }
   229	
   230	  const { bundle, warnings, unsafe } = loadBundle(root, io);
   231	  for (const w of warnings) io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: ${w}\n`);
   232	
   233	  // An unsafe managed tree (symlinked .handoff — gate-2 fix 6) is NOT "no
   234	  // bundle yet": seeding here would write through the link. Refuse everything;
   235	  // hook-safety keeps the refusal soft outside --strict.
   236	  if (unsafe === true) {
   237	    io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: managed tree failed the symlink/realpath jail — nothing written\n');
   238	    return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'unsafe-tree', msg: warnings[0] ?? 'managed tree failed the jail check' } }, strict ? 1 : 0);
   239	  }
   240	
   241	  // Opt-in debounce (gate-2 major 14; Cursor afterFileEdit fires per edit):
   242	  // at most one real checkpoint per window, tracked by a per-platform stamp.
   243	  // The read-and-set runs UNDER the repo lock (gate-2 iter-2 M5) so two
   244	  // simultaneous afterFileEdit hooks can't both observe no stamp and both
   245	  // checkpoint — exactly one wins the window.
   246	  if (flags.debounce !== undefined) {
   247	    const seconds = Number(flags.debounce);
   248	    if (!Number.isFinite(seconds) || seconds <= 0) return usageError(io, flags, 'checkpoint', '--debounce <seconds> must be a positive number');
   249	    const stampPath = `${paths.logDir}/debounce-${platform}.json`;
   250	    const proceed = withLock(root, io, () => {
   251	      const stamp = safeReadJson(io.fs, stampPath);
   252	      if (stamp.ok && typeof stamp.value?.at === 'string' && Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(stamp.value.at) < seconds * 1000) {
   253	        return false;
   254	      }
   255	      ensureDir(io.fs, paths.logDir);
   256	      atomicWriteJson(io.fs, stampPath, { at: io.now() });
   257	      return true;
   258	    });
   259	    if (!proceed) return finish({ ok: true, data: { events: 0, rewritten: false, debounced: true } }, 0);
   260	  }
   261	
   262	  let active = bundle;
   263	  let mustRewrite = false;
   264	
   265	  // Semantic isolation (plan §Concurrency): a stable foreign session or a
   266	  // mismatched origin platform must not merge into the active bundle. Checked
   267	  // before ANY write so a rejection leaves the whole tree untouched.
   268	  const originHint = active?.origin?.sessionHint;
   269	  const hasStableOwner = active !== null && typeof originHint === 'string' && originHint.length > 0 && active.origin.unstable !== true;
   270	  const stableIncoming = events.find(
   271	    (/** @type {any} */ e) => typeof e.sessionHint === 'string' && e.sessionHint.length > 0 && e.unstable === false,
   272	  );
   273	  const unstableIncoming = events.some((/** @type {any} */ e) => e.unstable === true);
   274	
   275	  if (hasStableOwner) {
   276	    // Foreign = a different stable session, OR any platform mismatch — the
   277	    // platform is provable even when the events carry no session hint (audit
   278	    // finding: a hint-less /baton:handoff narrative from claude-code silently
   279	    // merged into — then sealed — a codex-owned bundle, corrupting the origin
   280	    // the receive-side role avoidance keys on).
   281	    const incomingLabel = stableIncoming ? stableIncoming.sessionHint : '(no session hint)';
   282	    const foreign = stableIncoming
   283	      ? stableIncoming.sessionHint !== originHint || platform !== active.origin.platform
   284	      : platform !== active.origin.platform;
   285	    if (foreign && flags['take-over'] !== true) {
   286	      io.stderr.write(
   287	        `baton checkpoint: event from a foreign session/origin (${platform}/${incomingLabel} vs active ${active.origin.platform}/${originHint}) — rejected to keep sessions isolated; rerun with --take-over to archive the active bundle and start fresh\n`,
   288	      );
   289	      return finish({ ok: false, error: { code: 'foreign-session', msg: 'event from a foreign session/origin rejected; rerun with --take-over' } }, 0);
   290	    }
   291	    if (foreign) {
   292	      rotateJournal(root, 'takeover', io);
   293	      const fresh = emptyBundle(
   294	        { platform, model: typeof flags.model === 'string' ? flags.model : 'unknown', goal: active.task?.goal ?? 'unknown' },
   295	        io.now(),
   296	      );
   297	      if (stableIncoming) fresh.origin.sessionHint = stableIncoming.sessionHint;
   298	      writeSnapshot(root, fresh, io);
   299	      io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: took over — prior bundle archived to history/, fresh bundle owned by ${platform}/${incomingLabel}\n`);
   300	      active = fresh;
   301	      mustRewrite = true;
   302	    }
   303	  }
   304	
   305	  if (unstableIncoming) {
   306	    io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: event carries an unstable (generated) session hint — ownership unverifiable; applied with this warning only\n');
   307	  }
   308	
   309	  if (active === null) {
   310	    active = emptyBundle(
   311	      { platform, model: typeof flags.model === 'string' ? flags.model : 'unknown', goal: 'unknown' },
   312	      io.now(),
   313	    );
   314	    writeSnapshot(root, active, io);
   315	    // Self-applying seed event (gate-2 iter-2 B6): a journal-only rebuild
   316	    // (snapshot + .bak both lost) must restore the bundle's identity, not the
   317	    // "unknown" placeholder resolveBase seeds. bundle.seed carries bundleId,
   318	    // origin, task, and generation and is applied on replay.
   319	    appendJournal(
   320	      root,
   321	      {
   322	        ts: io.now(),
   323	        type: 'bundle.seed',
   324	        payload: {
   325	          bundleId: active.bundleId,
   326	          generation: active.generation,
   327	          createdAt: active.createdAt,
   328	          origin: active.origin,
   329	          task: { goal: active.task.goal },
   330	          text: `[seed] auto-seeded a new bundle on ${platform} (model ${active.origin.model})`,
   331	        },
   332	        writerId: [platform, io.pid, 'seed'].join('-'),
   333	        dedupeKey: dedupeKey({ ts: io.now(), type: 'bundle.seed', bundleId: active.bundleId }),
   334	      },
   335	      io,
   336	    );
   337	    io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: no bundle found — auto-seeded a new one at .handoff/bundle.json\n');
   338	    mustRewrite = true;
   339	  }
   340	
   341	  // Ownership adoption (gate-2 fix 2): a bundle whose owner is unverified — a
   342	  // fresh seed (null hint) or a receive that defaulted to an unstable hint —
   343	  // adopts the first STABLE same-platform session as its owner, so semantic
   344	  // isolation engages from then on instead of never.
   345	  const ownerUnverified = active.origin.sessionHint === null || active.origin.unstable === true;
   346	  if (ownerUnverified && stableIncoming && active.origin.platform === platform) {
   347	    active = { ...active, origin: { ...active.origin, sessionHint: stableIncoming.sessionHint, unstable: false } };
   348	    writeSnapshot(root, active, io);
   349	    io.stderr.write(`baton checkpoint: adopted ${platform}/${stableIncoming.sessionHint} as the bundle owner (first verified session)\n`);
   350	    mustRewrite = true;
   351	  }
   352	
   353	  let lastSeq = 0;
   354	  for (const ev of events) {
   355	    const ts = typeof ev.ts === 'string' ? ev.ts : io.now();
   356	    const entry = {
   357	      ...ev,
   358	      ts,
   359	      dedupeKey:
   360	        typeof ev.dedupeKey === 'string' && ev.dedupeKey.length > 0
   361	          ? ev.dedupeKey
   362	          : dedupeKey({ ts, type: ev.type, payload: ev.payload, source: ev.source, sessionHint: ev.sessionHint }),
   363	      writerId: [platform, io.pid, ev.sessionHint ?? 'local'].join('-'),
   364	    };
   365	    lastSeq = appendJournal(root, entry, io);
   366	  }
   367	
   368	  const snap = safeReadJson(io.fs, paths.snapshot);
   369	  const snapSeq = snap.ok && typeof snap.value?.journalSeq === 'number' ? snap.value.journalSeq : 0;
   370	  const snapUpdated = snap.ok && typeof snap.value?.updatedAt === 'string' ? Date.parse(snap.value.updatedAt) : 0;
   371	  const important = events.some((/** @type {any} */ e) => !ROUTINE_TYPES.has(e.type));
   372	  const elapsed = Date.parse(io.now()) - snapUpdated > THROTTLE_WINDOW_MS;
   373	  const accreted = lastSeq - snapSeq;
   374	
   375	  let rewritten = false;
   376	  if (mustRewrite || important || elapsed || accreted >= THROTTLE_EVENT_COUNT) {
   377	    // Bounded git refresh on EVERY snapshot rewrite (gate-2 iter-2 M1): a
   378	    // mechanical checkpoint carries branch/HEAD/dirty. Capture git BEFORE the
   379	    // lock — it is async and must not straddle the lock — then do ONE atomic
   380	    // read-modify-write UNDER the lock (iter-5 A1): reload the bundle inside the
   381	    // lock, apply git, and write with the fencing token. The old code loaded
   382	    // outside the lock, awaited git (yielding the event loop), then wrote the
   383	    // stale bundle, so a concurrent --take-over during the await was clobbered
   384	    // (origin rolled back, the new owner's event lost). Reloading inside the
   385	    // lock guarantees the write is built from current state.
   386	    const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
   387	    rewritten = withLock(root, io, (token) => {
   388	      const merged = loadBundle(root, io).bundle;
   389	      if (!merged) return false;
   390	      if (git !== null) {
   391	        merged.git = git;
   392	      } else {
   393	        // git refresh failed/timed out (iter-4 I2/F5): NEVER retain the prior
   394	        // snapshot — stale HEAD/dirty presented as current corrupts the
   395	        // receive-side evidence audit. Clear to explicit unavailable (null) and
   396	        // warn; a repo that was never git (already null) warns nothing.
   397	        if (merged.git !== null) {
   398	          io.stderr.write('baton checkpoint: git refresh failed — clearing stale git state (marked unavailable; receive re-derives it live)\n');
   399	        }
   400	        merged.git = null;
   401	      }
   402	      writeSnapshotIn(root, merged, io, token);
   403	      return true;
   404	    });
   405	  }
   406	  return finish({ ok: true, data: { events: events.length, rewritten } }, 0);
   407	}

     8	const RESUME_HINT = {
     9	  'claude-code': 'Suggest running /baton:receive to resume that task with remapped roles.',
    10	  codex: "Run 'baton receive --platform codex' or the baton-handoff skill to resume that task with remapped roles.",
    11	  cursor: "Run 'baton receive --platform cursor' to resume that task with remapped roles.",

    14	/**
    15	 * `baton session-start` — the cheap SessionStart gate the adapters call
    16	 * instead of full receive preparation (gate-2 fix 8). Inspects only
    17	 * pending-foreign state: a bundle that is sealed OR limit-hit AND from
    18	 * another platform yields a harness-shaped notice on stdout; every other state
    19	 * — own bundle, open non-limit bundle, no bundle, corrupt tree — is a quiet
    20	 * no-op. Soft paths ALWAYS exit 0 (a session-start notice must never break a
    21	 * session); a mis-parsed invocation is a broken hook manifest, which is loud
    22	 * (exit 2) so init/doctor drift gets noticed. With --json (surface-audit fold)
    23	 * the standard envelope wraps the outcome so machine consumers can tell "no
    24	 * pending handoff" from a swallowed failure: data = {pending, shaped}.
    25	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    26	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    27	 */
    28	export async function cmdSessionStart(args, io) {
    29	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string' });
    30	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'session-start', parsed.error);
    31	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    32	  const finish = (/** @type {boolean} */ pending, /** @type {any} */ shaped) => {
    33	    if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { pending, shaped } });
    34	    else if (shaped !== null) io.stdout.write(typeof shaped === 'string' ? `${shaped}\n` : JSON.stringify(shaped) + '\n');
    35	    return 0;
    36	  };
    37	  try {
    38	    const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    39	    if (!platform || platformError(platform) !== null) {
    40	      io.stderr.write('baton session-start: --platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required — emitting nothing\n');
    41	      return finish(false, null);
    42	    }
    43	
    44	    const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    45	    // Full load (journal replay included): an unsealed limit death may live
    46	    // only as a post-snapshot journal note. Load warnings are deliberately
    47	    // not surfaced — this is a notice path, not a diagnostics path. An UNSAFE
    48	    // managed tree (symlinked .handoff — threat model / F12) is a quiet no-op:
    49	    // never emit context derived from a tree that failed the jail.
    50	    const { bundle, unsafe } = loadBundle(root, io);
    51	    if (bundle === null || unsafe === true) return finish(false, null);
    52	
    53	    const origin = bundle?.origin?.platform;
    54	    const pending = bundle?.handoff?.status === 'sealed' || bundle?.handoff?.reasonClass === 'usage-limit';
    55	    const foreign = typeof origin === 'string' && origin !== platform;
    56	    if (!pending || !foreign) return finish(false, null);
    57	
    58	    const kind = bundle.handoff.status === 'sealed' ? 'sealed' : 'limit-hit before sealing';
    59	    // The bundle is untrusted input (threat model, iter-3 F12): allowlist the
    60	    // origin label — never interpolate a crafted origin string into injected
    61	    // session context.
    62	    const label = typeof origin === 'string' && platformError(origin) === null ? origin : 'another platform';
    63	    const msg = `A handoff bundle from ${label} is pending in .handoff/ (${kind}). ${RESUME_HINT[platform] ?? RESUME_HINT.codex}`;
    64	
    65	    if (platform === 'cursor') return finish(true, { additional_context: msg });
    66	    if (platform === 'claude-code') return finish(true, { hookSpecificOutput: { hookEventName: 'SessionStart', additionalContext: msg } });
    67	    return finish(true, msg);
    68	  } catch {
    69	    return finish(false, null);
    70	  }

     1	// Single Claude Code hook entrypoint. hooks.json routes every event here with
     2	// the event name as argv[0]; this script's only job is to reach the core CLI
     3	// (never re-implement it) and to stay fail-open: a hook must never break the
     4	// host session, so every path returns 0.
     5	
     6	import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
     7	import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
     8	import * as nodeFs from 'node:fs';
     9	import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
    10	import { checkHandoffTree } from '../../../core/src/util/jail.mjs';
    11	
    12	/** @param {any} io */
    13	const batonBin = (io) => `${io.env?.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT ?? '.'}/core/bin/baton.mjs`;
    14	
    15	/**
    16	 * execFile-shaped spawn that supports {input}: async execFile has no input
    17	 * option, and a child reading stdin would otherwise block forever (gate-2
    18	 * reviewer-b finding 1). The io contract for this adapter is therefore
    19	 * "promise execFile honoring opts.input as the child's stdin".
    20	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string[]} args @param {{cwd?: string, input?: string, timeout?: number}} [opts]
    21	 * @returns {Promise<{stdout: string, stderr: string}>}
    22	 */
    23	function execFileWithInput(cmd, args = [], opts = {}) {
    24	  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    25	    const child = spawn(cmd, args, { cwd: opts.cwd, timeout: opts.timeout ?? 25_000 });
    26	    let stdout = '';
    27	    let stderr = '';
    28	    child.stdout.on('data', (d) => (stdout += d));
    29	    child.stderr.on('data', (d) => (stderr += d));
    30	    child.on('error', reject);
    31	    child.on('close', (code) => {
    32	      if (code === 0) return resolve({ stdout, stderr });
    33	      const e = Object.assign(new Error(`Command failed: ${cmd}`), { code, stdout, stderr });
    34	      reject(e);
    35	    });
    36	    // Fail-open (audit finding 21): a child that dies without draining a large
    37	    // stdin payload emits an out-of-band EPIPE 'error' on stdin that bypasses
    38	    // every try/catch and crashes the hook with a stack trace. Swallow it —
    39	    // the close handler still reports the child's real exit.
    40	    child.stdin?.on('error', () => {});
    41	    if (typeof opts.input === 'string' && child.stdin) child.stdin.write(opts.input);
    42	    child.stdin?.end();
    43	  });
    44	}

    46	// Hook-error diagnostics (gate-2 minor 16 / plan §Transcript policy log
    47	// contract): metadata-only entries — never the hook payload — with bounded
    48	// retention (last 50 entries / 7 days). The log rides .handoff/ so the purge
    49	// walk covers it.
    50	const HOOK_LOG_MAX = 50;
    51	const HOOK_LOG_MAX_AGE_MS = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
    52	
    53	/** @param {any} io @param {string} event @param {any} err */
    54	function logHookError(io, event, err) {
    55	  try {
    56	    // The diagnostics log rides .handoff/, so it passes the same managed-tree
    57	    // jail as every other write (threat model): refuse to create or write
    58	    // through a symlinked/escaped .handoff. Diagnostics must never be the hole
    59	    // the jail closes elsewhere — and this stays fail-open (skip, don't throw).
    60	    if (!checkHandoffTree(io.cwd, io).ok) return;
    61	    const dir = `${io.cwd}/.handoff/log`;
    62	    io.fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    63	    const path = `${dir}/hook-errors.jsonl`;
    64	    const nowIso = typeof io.now === 'function' ? io.now() : new Date().toISOString();
    65	    /** @type {any[]} */
    66	    let entries = [];
    67	    try {
    68	      entries = io.fs
    69	        .readFileSync(path, 'utf8')
    70	        .split('\n')
    71	        .filter((/** @type {string} */ l) => l.trim() !== '')
    72	        .map((/** @type {string} */ l) => {
    73	          try {
    74	            return JSON.parse(l);
    75	          } catch {
    76	            return null;
    77	          }
    78	        })
    79	        .filter(Boolean);
    80	    } catch {
    81	      entries = [];
    82	    }
    83	    entries.push({ ts: nowIso, event, code: err?.code ?? null });
    84	    const cutoff = Date.parse(nowIso) - HOOK_LOG_MAX_AGE_MS;
    85	    entries = entries.filter((e) => typeof e.ts === 'string' && Date.parse(e.ts) >= cutoff).slice(-HOOK_LOG_MAX);
    86	    io.fs.writeFileSync(path, entries.map((e) => JSON.stringify(e)).join('\n') + '\n');
    87	  } catch {
    88	    // diagnostics must never break fail-open
    89	  }
    90	}
    91	
    92	/**
    93	 * Run a core baton command as a child process, forwarding the raw hook payload
    94	 * as the child's stdin. Rejections are swallowed (fail-open) — core commands
    95	 * carry their own hook-safety, and Claude ignores hook exit codes anyway —
    96	 * but each failure leaves a metadata-only diagnostic in .handoff/log/.
    97	 * opts.acceptCodes: exit codes that are VERDICTS, not failures (audit finding
    98	 * 20: detect's 10/11/12/13 classification exits were logged as hook errors on
    99	 * every real limit death, drowning the diagnostics log in noise).
   100	 * @param {any} io @param {string[]} argv @param {string} event
   101	 * @param {{timeout?: number, acceptCodes?: number[]}} [opts]
   102	 * @returns {Promise<{stdout: string, stderr: string} | null>}
   103	 */
   104	async function baton(io, argv, event, opts = {}) {
   105	  try {
   106	    return await io.execFile('node', [batonBin(io), ...argv], { input: io.stdin, timeout: opts.timeout });
   107	  } catch (err) {
   108	    if (Array.isArray(opts.acceptCodes) && opts.acceptCodes.includes(/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code)) {
   109	      const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   110	      return { stdout: e?.stdout ?? '', stderr: e?.stderr ?? '' };
   111	    }
   112	    // fail-open: a failing child must not break the session
   113	    logHookError(io, event, err);
   114	    return null;
   115	  }
   116	}
   117	
   118	/** @param {any} io @returns {any | null} parsed hook payload, or null */
   119	function parsePayload(io) {
   120	  try {
   121	    return JSON.parse(typeof io.stdin === 'string' ? io.stdin : '');
   122	  } catch {
   123	    return null;
   124	  }
   125	}
   126	
   127	/**
   128	 * SessionStart: not a checkpoint — delegate to the core `session-start`
   129	 * command (audit finding 19: the old inline version read
   130	 * `${io.cwd}/.handoff/bundle.json` raw, so launching Claude Code in a repo
   131	 * SUBDIRECTORY silently never fired the pending-handoff notice while the same
   132	 * session's Stop checkpoints DID land at the discovered toplevel). Core owns
   133	 * root discovery, the managed-tree jail, and the origin allowlist; this shim
   134	 * only forwards the shaped stdout the harness consumes.
   135	 * @param {any} io
   136	 */
   137	async function sessionStart(io) {
   138	  const r = await baton(io, ['session-start', '--platform', 'claude-code'], 'SessionStart', { timeout: 8_000 });
   139	  if (r && typeof r.stdout === 'string' && r.stdout.length > 0) io.stdout.write(r.stdout);
   140	  return 0;
   141	}
   142	
   143	/**
   144	 * @param {string[]} args argv after the script path; args[0] is the hook event
   145	 * @param {any} io injected {cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now, …}
   146	 * @returns {Promise<number>} always 0 — fail-open by construction
   147	 */
   148	export async function runHook(args, io) {
   149	  try {
   150	    const event = args[0] ?? 'unknown';
   151	
   152	    if (event === 'SessionStart') return sessionStart(io);
   153	
   154	    if (event === 'StopFailure') {
   155	      // CHECKPOINT FIRST (audit finding 22): it is the part that must land
   156	      // before the harness's 30s envelope kills the hook — the limit-death
   157	      // checkpoint is the whole point of this event. Explicit per-child
   158	      // timeouts sum under the envelope (18s + 8s < 30s). detect runs second,
   159	      // and its classification exit codes are VERDICTS, not errors (finding
   160	      // 20) — only real failures (1/2/spawn) reach the diagnostics log.
   161	      await baton(io, ['checkpoint', '--platform', 'claude-code', '--trigger', 'StopFailure'], event, { timeout: 18_000 });
   162	      const errorType = parsePayload(io)?.error?.type;
   163	      const detectArgs = ['detect', '--platform', 'claude-code'];
   164	      if (typeof errorType === 'string') detectArgs.push('--structured-error-type', errorType);
   165	      await baton(io, detectArgs, event, { timeout: 8_000, acceptCodes: [0, 10, 11, 12, 13] });
   166	      return 0;
   167	    }
   168	
   169	    // Stop / PreCompact / SessionEnd — and any future event — are routine
   170	    // mechanical checkpoints; core normalize degrades unknown payloads safely.
   171	    await baton(io, ['checkpoint', '--platform', 'claude-code'], event);
   172	    return 0;
   173	  } catch {
   174	    return 0;
   175	  }
   176	}
   177	
   178	// Main entry: hooks.json executes this file directly (`node …/hook.mjs <Event>`),
   179	// so a real invocation must build the real io and run — an export alone is a
   180	// production no-op (gate-2 reviewer-b finding 1).
   181	const isMain = (() => {
   182	  try {
   183	    return process.argv[1] ? import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href : false;
   184	  } catch {
   185	    return false;
   186	  }
   187	})();
   188	
   189	if (isMain) {
   190	  let stdin = '';
   191	  try {
   192	    if (!process.stdin.isTTY) stdin = readFileSync(0, 'utf8');
   193	  } catch {
   194	    stdin = '';
   195	  }
   196	  const io = {
   197	    cwd: process.cwd(),
   198	    env: process.env,
   199	    stdin,
   200	    stdout: process.stdout,
   201	    stderr: process.stderr,
   202	    fs: nodeFs,
   203	    execFile: execFileWithInput,
   204	    now: () => new Date().toISOString(),
   205	  };
   206	  runHook(process.argv.slice(2), io).then(
   207	    (code) => process.exit(code),
   208	    () => process.exit(0),
   209	  );
   210	}

     1	// In-memory fs fake for unit tests. Synchronous subset only.
     2	// Atomicity contract: renameSync moves content in a single step, so a target
     3	// path is never observed carrying partial content. Every mutation appends a
     4	// full snapshot to `fs.__history`, letting tests enumerate intermediate states
     5	// and prove the tmp+rename discipline (target's first appearance is a rename).
     6	
     7	function fsError(code, syscall, path) {
     8	  const e = new Error(`${code}: ${syscall} '${path}'`);
     9	  e.code = code;
    10	  e.syscall = syscall;
    11	  e.path = path;
    12	  return e;
    13	}
    14	
    15	function normalize(p) {
    16	  const str = String(p);
    17	  const isAbs = str.startsWith('/');
    18	  const out = [];
    19	  for (const part of str.split('/')) {
    20	    if (part === '' || part === '.') continue;
    21	    if (part === '..') {
    22	      out.pop();
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    out.push(part);
    26	  }
    27	  return (isAbs ? '/' : '') + out.join('/');
    28	}
    29	
    30	function dirname(np) {
    31	  const i = np.lastIndexOf('/');
    32	  if (i <= 0) return '/';
    33	  return np.slice(0, i);
    34	}
    35	
    36	function basename(np, parent) {
    37	  return parent === '/' ? np.slice(1) : np.slice(parent.length + 1);
    38	}
    39	
    40	export function makeMemfs(initialFiles = {}) {
    41	  const store = new Map(); // path -> string content
    42	  const dirs = new Set(['/']);
    43	  const mtimes = new Map();
    44	  const history = [];
    45	  let clock = 0;
    46	
    47	  const snapshot = () => Object.fromEntries(store);
    48	  const record = (op, paths) => history.push({ op, paths, files: snapshot() });
    49	  const touch = (np) => mtimes.set(np, ++clock);
    50	
    51	  const mkdirp = (np) => {
    52	    let cur = '';
    53	    for (const part of np.split('/').filter(Boolean)) {
    54	      cur = cur + '/' + part;
    55	      dirs.add(cur);
    56	    }
    57	  };
    58	
    59	  const ensureParent = (np, syscall) => {
    60	    const parent = dirname(np);
    61	    if (parent === '/' ) return;
    62	    if (!dirs.has(parent)) throw fsError('ENOENT', syscall, np);
    63	  };
    64	
    65	  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(initialFiles)) {
    66	    const np = normalize(p);
    67	    mkdirp(dirname(np));
    68	    store.set(np, String(content));
    69	    touch(np);
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  const fs = {
    73	    readFileSync(p /*, enc */) {
    74	      const np = normalize(p);
    75	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'open', np);
    76	      return store.get(np);
    77	    },
    78	
    79	    writeFileSync(p, data /*, enc */) {
    80	      const np = normalize(p);
    81	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    82	      store.set(np, String(data));
    83	      touch(np);
    84	      record('writeFileSync', [np]);
    85	    },
    86	
    87	    appendFileSync(p, data) {
    88	      const np = normalize(p);
    89	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    90	      store.set(np, (store.get(np) || '') + String(data));
    91	      touch(np);
    92	      record('appendFileSync', [np]);
    93	    },
    94	
    95	    renameSync(from, to) {
    96	      const nf = normalize(from);
    97	      const nt = normalize(to);
    98	      if (store.has(nf)) {
    99	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   100	        const content = store.get(nf);
   101	        store.delete(nf);
   102	        store.set(nt, content);
   103	        touch(nt);
   104	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   105	        return;
   106	      }
   107	      // Directory rename (atomic move of the node and all descendants) — the
   108	      // lock's dead-reclaim arbitration renames the stale lock dir aside.
   109	      if (dirs.has(nf)) {
   110	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   111	        const pre = nf + '/';
   112	        dirs.delete(nf);
   113	        dirs.add(nt);
   114	        for (const k of [...store.keys()]) {
   115	          if (k.startsWith(pre)) {
   116	            store.set(nt + k.slice(nf.length), store.get(k));
   117	            store.delete(k);
   118	          }
   119	        }
   120	        for (const d of [...dirs]) {
   121	          if (d !== nt && d.startsWith(pre)) {
   122	            dirs.add(nt + d.slice(nf.length));
   123	            dirs.delete(d);
   124	          }
   125	        }
   126	        touch(nt);
   127	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   128	        return;
   129	      }
   130	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rename', nf);
   131	    },
   132	
   133	    mkdirSync(p, opts = {}) {
   134	      const np = normalize(p);
   135	      if (opts.recursive) {
   136	        mkdirp(np);
   137	        record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   138	        return undefined;
   139	      }
   140	      ensureParent(np, 'mkdir');
   141	      if (dirs.has(np) || store.has(np)) throw fsError('EEXIST', 'mkdir', np);
   142	      dirs.add(np);
   143	      record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   144	      return undefined;
   145	    },
   146	
   147	    existsSync(p) {
   148	      const np = normalize(p);
   149	      return store.has(np) || dirs.has(np);
   150	    },
   151	
   152	    readdirSync(p) {
   153	      const np = normalize(p);
   154	      if (!dirs.has(np)) {
   155	        if (store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOTDIR', 'scandir', np);
   156	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'scandir', np);
   157	      }
   158	      const names = new Set();
   159	      const collect = (k) => {
   160	        if (k !== np && dirname(k) === np) names.add(basename(k, np));
   161	      };
   162	      for (const k of store.keys()) collect(k);
   163	      for (const d of dirs) collect(d);
   164	      return [...names];
   165	    },
   166	
   167	    unlinkSync(p) {
   168	      const np = normalize(p);
   169	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'unlink', np);
   170	      store.delete(np);
   171	      record('unlinkSync', [np]);
   172	    },
   173	
   174	    rmSync(p, opts = {}) {
   175	      const np = normalize(p);
   176	      const isFile = store.has(np);
   177	      const isDir = dirs.has(np);
   178	      if (!isFile && !isDir) {
   179	        if (opts.force) return;
   180	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rm', np);
   181	      }
   182	      if (isFile) store.delete(np);
   183	      if (isDir) {
   184	        const pre = np + '/';
   185	        if (!opts.recursive) {
   186	          const hasChildren = [...store.keys(), ...dirs].some((k) => k !== np && k.startsWith(pre));
   187	          if (hasChildren) throw fsError('ENOTEMPTY', 'rmdir', np);
   188	          dirs.delete(np);
   189	        } else {
   190	          for (const k of [...store.keys()]) if (k === np || k.startsWith(pre)) store.delete(k);
   191	          for (const d of [...dirs]) if (d === np || d.startsWith(pre)) dirs.delete(d);
   192	        }
   193	      }
   194	      record('rmSync', [np]);
   195	    },
   196	
   197	    statSync(p) {
   198	      const np = normalize(p);
   199	      if (store.has(np)) {
   200	        const size = Buffer.byteLength(store.get(np));
   201	        return { isFile: () => true, isDirectory: () => false, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   202	      }
   203	      if (dirs.has(np)) {
   204	        return { isFile: () => false, isDirectory: () => true, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size: 0, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   205	      }
   206	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'stat', np);
   207	    },
   208	
   209	    // The fake cannot represent symlinks, so lstat === stat and realpath is
   210	    // normalization. Symlink-refusal behavior is proven over the real fs in
   211	    // tests/integration/symlink-escape.test.mjs.
   212	    lstatSync(p) {
   213	      return fs.statSync(p);
   214	    },
   215	
   216	    realpathSync(p) {
   217	      const np = normalize(p);
   218	      if (!store.has(np) && !dirs.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'realpath', np);
   219	      return np;
   220	    },
   221	
   222	    copyFileSync(src, dest) {
   223	      const ns = normalize(src);
   224	      const nd = normalize(dest);
   225	      if (!store.has(ns)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'copyfile', ns);
   226	      ensureParent(nd, 'copyfile');
   227	      store.set(nd, store.get(ns));
   228	      touch(nd);
   229	      record('copyFileSync', [ns, nd]);
   230	    },
   231	  };
   232	
   233	  // Introspection hook for atomicity assertions; not part of the real fs API.
   234	  fs.__history = history;
   235	
   236	  return { fs, files: () => snapshot() };
   237	}

    29	function lockPaths(root) {
    30	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff`;
    31	  return {
    32	    dir,
    33	    lockDir: `${dir}/lock`,
    34	    owner: `${dir}/lock/owner.json`,
    35	    journal: `${dir}/journal.ndjson`,
    36	  };

    39	const ESCAPE_HATCH =
    40	  'Verify manually that no baton process still holds it, then delete .handoff/lock to clear.';
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Inspect the current lock state.
    44	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p
    45	 * @returns {{state: 'free'} | {state: 'torn', cause: string} | {state: 'live'|'dead'|'cross-host', owner: any}}
    46	 */
    47	function inspect(io, p) {
    48	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.lockDir)) return { state: 'free' };
    49	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.owner)) {
    50	    return { state: 'torn', cause: 'owner.json is missing (torn lock metadata)' };
    51	  }
    52	  let owner;
    53	  try {
    54	    owner = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
    55	  } catch {
    56	    return { state: 'torn', cause: 'owner.json is unparseable (torn lock metadata)' };
    57	  }
    58	  if (owner.host !== io.host) return { state: 'cross-host', owner };
    59	  if (io.processAlive(owner.pid, owner.startTime)) return { state: 'live', owner };
    60	  return { state: 'dead', owner };
    61	}

    63	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p @param {string} text */
    64	function journalNote(io, p, text) {
    65	  io.fs.mkdirSync(p.dir, { recursive: true });
    66	  // Allocate seq + dedupeKey like every other journal entry (gate-2 fix,
    67	  // reviewer-b finding 2): a keyless, seq-less note poisons journalSeq to NaN
    68	  // on replay and dedupes every later keyless event. We are already inside the
    69	  // lock here, so reading the tail for allocation is race-free.
    70	  let tailSeq = 0;
    71	  try {
    72	    const snap = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(`${p.dir}/bundle.json`, 'utf8'));
    73	    if (typeof snap?.journalSeq === 'number') tailSeq = snap.journalSeq;
    74	  } catch {
    75	    // no snapshot yet — journal tail below still counts
    76	  }
    77	  try {
    78	    for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
    79	      if (typeof e.seq === 'number' && e.seq > tailSeq) tailSeq = e.seq;
    80	    }
    81	  } catch {
    82	    // no journal yet
    83	  }
    84	  const ts = io.now();
    85	  appendEntry(io.fs, p.journal, {
    86	    seq: tailSeq + 1,
    87	    ts,
    88	    type: 'note',
    89	    dedupeKey: dedupeKey({ ts, type: 'note', source: 'lock', text }),
    90	    writerId: ['lock', io.host, io.pid].join('-'),
    91	    source: 'lock',
    92	    payload: { text },
    93	  });
    94	}

    96	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p */
    97	function publishOwner(io, p) {
    98	  const token = io.newFencingToken();
    99	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, p.owner, {
   100	    host: io.host,

   103	    fencingToken: token,
   104	    acquiredAt: io.now(),

   108	}

   110	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p */
   111	function acquire(io, p) {
   112	  io.fs.mkdirSync(p.dir, { recursive: true });
   113	  try {
   114	    io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   115	    return publishOwner(io, p);
   116	  } catch (err) {
   117	    if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code !== 'EEXIST') throw err;
   118	  }
   119	
   120	  const s = inspect(io, p);
   121	  switch (s.state) {
   122	    case 'free':
   123	      // Raced a release between mkdir failure and inspection; retry once. A
   124	      // competitor can win THIS mkdir too (gate-2 fix 12: observed under real
   125	      // barrier-released contention) — that is contention, not corruption.
   126	      try {
   127	        io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   128	      } catch (err) {
   129	        if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'EEXIST') {
   130	          throw new LockHeldError('lock was re-acquired by a competitor mid-inspection — transient contention');
   131	        }
   132	        throw err;
   133	      }
   134	      return publishOwner(io, p);
   135	    case 'live':
   136	      throw new LockHeldError(
   137	        `lock is held by a live process (pid ${s.owner.pid} on ${s.owner.host}) — terminate that process first.`,
   138	      );
   139	    case 'cross-host':
   140	      throw new Error(
   141	        `lock is held from another host ('${s.owner.host}', this is '${io.host}') — cross-host locks are never provably safe to steal. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`,
   142	      );
   143	    case 'torn':
   144	      // During ACQUIRE, torn metadata is usually a competitor caught between
   145	      // its mkdir and owner.json publication — transient by construction, so
   146	      // LockHeldError lets withLock wait it out (gate-2 fix 12). A genuinely
   147	      // crashed publisher exhausts the retry budget and surfaces this same
   148	      // message; recovery semantics (recoverLock) are unchanged: never steal.
   149	      throw new LockHeldError(`${s.cause} — unsupported for automatic recovery. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`);
   150	    case 'dead': {
   151	      // Atomic dead-lock reclaim (gate-2 iter-2 B1, hardened iter-3 finding-1).
   152	      // rm-then-mkdir is NOT mutually exclusive: two contenders can both observe
   153	      // 'dead' before either removes the stale dir, then both rm+mkdir — the
   154	      // second silently clobbers the first, yielding two simultaneous holders and
   155	      // two takeover notes. rename() is the atomic arbiter instead: the stale
   156	      // lock dir has exactly one name, so only one process can move it aside (the
   157	      // rest get ENOENT). Winning the rename IS winning the reclaim; a contender
   158	      // that recreated the dir via the 'free' path first makes our mkdir EEXIST →
   159	      // transient contention. The fencing token stays the write-time backstop.
   160	      const aside = `${p.lockDir}.reclaim-${io.pid}-${io.newFencingToken()}`;
   161	      try {
   162	        io.fs.renameSync(p.lockDir, aside);
   163	      } catch (err) {
   164	        // Source already gone → another contender is reclaiming it; wait+retry.
   165	        if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'ENOENT') {
   166	          throw new LockHeldError('a competitor is reclaiming the dead lock — transient contention');
   167	        }
   168	        throw err;
   169	      }
   170	      io.fs.rmSync(aside, { recursive: true, force: true });
   171	      try {
   172	        io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   173	      } catch (err) {
   174	        if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'EEXIST') {
   175	          throw new LockHeldError('a competitor re-claimed the dead lock first — transient contention');
   176	        }
   177	        throw err;
   178	      }
   179	      const token = publishOwner(io, p);
   180	      // Journal the takeover only now — we HOLD the lock (fresh dir + published
   181	      // owner), so the note's seq is allocated while holding it (plan
   182	      // §Concurrency "sequence numbers are allocated under the lock"), never
   183	      // racing a free-path competitor's concurrent append (iter-3 F2). The note
   184	      // is best-effort (0-or-1) and MUST NOT abort acquisition (iter-4 I7): if it
   185	      // threw here — after publishOwner, before the token is returned to withLock
   186	      // — the release finally would never run and the lock would leak with a live
   187	      // owner. Swallow it; the reclaim already succeeded.
   188	      try {
   189	        journalNote(io, p, `lock takeover: took over stale lock from dead pid ${s.owner.pid} (start time ${s.owner.startTime})`);
   190	      } catch {
   191	        // audit note is best-effort
   192	      }
   193	      return token;
   194	    }
   195	    /* c8 ignore next 2 */
   196	    default:
   197	      throw new Error('unreachable lock state');
   198	  }
   199	}
   200	
   201	/**
   202	 * Fence-guarded release: the lock dir is removed only while owner.json still
   203	 * carries OUR token. A holder that paused past a takeover must not delete the
   204	 * competitor's lock on its way out — release is a write like any other.
   205	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p @param {string} token
   206	 */
   207	function release(io, p, token) {
   208	  try {
   209	    const current = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
   210	    if (current.fencingToken !== token) return; // a competitor took over — their lock, not ours
   211	  } catch {
   212	    return; // owner metadata gone or torn mid-hold — never guess; leave it for recovery
   213	  }
   214	  io.fs.rmSync(p.lockDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
   215	}
   216	
   217	// A live same-host holder is transient by design — operation-scoped locks are
   218	// held for milliseconds — so contention waits instead of dropping the
   219	// operation (gate-2 fix 12: simultaneous checkpoints must all land). The budget
   220	// is generous (≈15 s): under heavy load a writer may be starved of scheduling
   221	// for seconds, and waiting is strictly safer than silently dropping its work.
   222	// Every other contended state (torn, cross-host) stays fail-fast: waiting
   223	// cannot make those provably safe.
   224	const DEFAULT_RETRY = { attempts: 600, delayMs: 25 };
   225	
   226	/** Synchronous sleep without burning a core: Atomics.wait on a throwaway buffer. */
   227	const sleepMs = (/** @type {number} */ ms) => {
   228	  Atomics.wait(new Int32Array(new SharedArrayBuffer(4)), 0, 0, ms);
   229	};
   230	
   231	/**
   232	 * Acquire → run fn(token) → release (also on throw). Synchronous by design:
   233	 * everything the lock guards is sync fs work. Live-held locks are retried on
   234	 * a bounded budget (override via io.lockRetry = {attempts, delayMs}).
   235	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {(token: string) => any} fn
   236	 */
   237	export function withLock(root, io, fn) {
   238	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   239	  // Jail BEFORE any lock mutation (gate-2 iter-2 B2): a symlinked .handoff or
   240	  // .handoff/lock must never be created into or removed through — that would
   241	  // reach outside the repository. Checked once here, ahead of acquire.
   242	  const safe = checkHandoffTree(root, io);
   243	  if (!safe.ok) throw new Error(`${safe.problem} — refusing to acquire the lock`);
   244	  const retry = io.lockRetry ?? DEFAULT_RETRY;
   245	  /** @type {string} */
   246	  let token;
   247	  for (let attempt = 0; ; attempt++) {
   248	    try {
   249	      token = acquire(io, p);
   250	      break;
   251	    } catch (err) {
   252	      if (err instanceof LockHeldError && attempt < retry.attempts) {
   253	        sleepMs(retry.delayMs);
   254	        continue;
   255	      }
   256	      throw err;
   257	    }
   258	  }
   259	  try {
   260	    return fn(token);
   261	  } finally {
   262	    release(io, p, token);
   263	  }
   264	}
   265	
   266	/**
   267	 * Fast-abort layer: re-read the published token immediately before a write; a
   268	 * holder that paused past its last check aborts instead of clobbering.
   269	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {string} token @param {() => any} fn
   270	 */
   271	export function guardedWrite(root, io, token, fn) {
   272	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   273	  let current = null;
   274	  try {
   275	    current = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
   276	  } catch {
   277	    throw new FencingError('lock owner metadata is gone — fencing check failed; aborting write');
   278	  }
   279	  if (current.fencingToken !== token) {
   280	    throw new FencingError(
   281	      `fencing token changed (held '${token}', current '${current.fencingToken}') — another process took over; aborting write`,
   282	    );
   283	  }
   284	  return fn();
   285	}
   286	
   287	/**
   288	 * Explicit recovery. `force` is an intent flag only — it never changes the
   289	 * decision: exactly one state recovers (provably-dead same-host owner).
   290	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {{force?: boolean}} [opts]
   291	 * @returns {{recovered: boolean, refusedReason: string | null}}
   292	 */
   293	export function recoverLock(root, io, opts = {}) {
   294	  void opts;
   295	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   296	  // Jail before recovery (gate-2 iter-2 B2): recoverLock removes the lock dir,
   297	  // so a symlinked managed tree must be refused first — even with force, a
   298	  // symlink is never provably safe to delete through.
   299	  const safe = checkHandoffTree(root, io);
   300	  if (!safe.ok) return { recovered: false, refusedReason: `${safe.problem} — refusing recovery` };
   301	  const s = inspect(io, p);
   302	  switch (s.state) {
   303	    case 'free':
   304	      return { recovered: true, refusedReason: null };
   305	    case 'dead': {
   306	      // Reclaim atomically via a single acquire() attempt + release, NOT an
   307	      // inspect-then-rmSync (iter-4 I1): the old bare rmSync had a TOCTOU — a
   308	      // competitor could win the rename-arbitrated dead-reclaim between this
   309	      // inspect() and the rmSync, publish a LIVE owner, and recovery would then
   310	      // delete a lock whose owner is alive. acquire() re-inspects atomically and
   311	      // shares the SAME rename arbitration, so recovery and a concurrent takeover
   312	      // are mutually exclusive: acquire either reclaims a still-dead lock (and
   313	      // journals the takeover under the lock) or throws because it is now
   314	      // live/torn — in which case we refuse rather than steal. release() then
   315	      // leaves the slot clear.
   316	      let token;
   317	      try {
   318	        token = acquire(io, p);
   319	      } catch (err) {
   320	        return { recovered: false, refusedReason: /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err) };
   321	      }
   322	      release(io, p, token);
   323	      return { recovered: true, refusedReason: null };
   324	    }
   325	    case 'live':

     1	import { atomicWriteText, safeReadJson, ensureDir, atomicWriteJson } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
     2	import { appendEntry, readAllTolerant } from '../util/jsonl.mjs';
